Anchor-bracketed re-triangulation only changes the SEED of landmark Variables; LM iterations over the full factor graph (including the post-anchor projection factors) pull landmarks back to the same local minimum regardless of seed. To break the local minimum, the projection factors themselves at T-class kfs need to be modified (downweighted, dropped, or replaced) — not just the landmark seeds' source poses. Lesson: seed-only perturbations of LM Variables do not escape basin

2026-04-28 | NEXT-ITERATION-NOTE: while debugging this iteration, identified a candidate NAMED BUG in the keypoint metric itself at scripts/track.py:947-955. The keypoint_offset (0.1095, 0.0, -0.018) is defined under GripperCfg ("trajectory_offset_in_gripper" per viz.py field name) and viz.py applies it to urdf_root_poses = body_pose . body_T_gripper_tag (gripper-tag frame). But scripts/track.py:947-955 applies it directly to body_pose (body frame), skipping the body→gripper transform. If body_T_gripper_tag is non-identity (it generally is), the keypoint metric measures a virtual point that's a different rigid offset per tracker, biasing kp values by ~|body_T_gripper_tag.t|. This may be a frame-confusion bug — fix by composing body_pose . body_T_gripper_tag before applying offset. Will explore as next iteration; expect this to be a metric-correctness fix similar to the prior instrumentation iteration (improvement = JSON kp values now reflect physical taped-point distance)

The first-PnP world frame is the "purest" anchor reference. kp_G improves because tracker-to-tracker world-frame consistency is better; kp_T regresses because trailing trajectory now uses a slightly-IMU-biased world frame. anc_med regresses because individual gap-exit kfs no longer match the (averaged) world frame as well as they did the first-PnP one

Better than stage 1 (which had +27%, +25%, +53%) — per-pixel depth helps over constant. But 2-pass abstraction is fundamentally wrong: pass-1's track set is already well-tuned (rotation predictor + parallax filter + min_track_length), pass-2 produces a DIFFERENT track set that disturbs the LM. Even with better depth lookup, the track set quality drops. The 2-pass-frontend approach is the wrong structure regardless of depth strategy. Committing to abandon the 2-pass-frontend angle.

Different from Huber (per-residual downweighting) — targets per-landmark consistency across all kfs. Different from prior trajectory-collision filter (spatial proximity) — variance picks up drifting tracks, not just close-to-body | files: scripts/track.py

The LM's Huber kernel + multi-view DLT triangulation already filter outliers effectively; per-landmark variance filter at these thresholds is essentially a no-op.

Loop continued past STUCK with extensive structural exploration. The kept change at kp_T=70.4mm appears to be a sharp local optimum surrounded by cliffs. Confirmed via 60+ iterations spanning: parameter sweeps, robust kernels (Cauchy/GemanMcClure/Tukey on visual+anchor), inter-tracker BetweenFactorPose3 (constant + per-kf prep-stage means), trailing absolute pose priors, ZUPT (default + relaxed), Dogleg optimizer, IMU σ retuning (gyro/accel noise + RW), cross-tracker σ tuning, gauge σ, anchor huber_k, anchor world prior, smoothness σ tuning (both directions both rotation+translation), keyframe spacing, init pair shared-track threshold, max_iterations, velocity prior, long-range smoothness, no-smoothness ablation, Cauchy/GemanMcClure(2,5,10)/Tukey(4) on visual factors, anchor-only Tukey, per-obs residual filter, per-tracker bias prior tightening, per-kf inter-tracker BetweenFactor with various means.

Robust kernel experiments revealed an anchor-vs-trajectory tradeoff: Cauchy/GemanMcClure visual reduce anc_med to 4-12mm (-25% to -75%) but inflate kp_T 70% to 100%. There's no single robust kernel that improves both.

The kp_T=70mm appears to require fundamental data: more anchor obs in tracker_0's trailing window, or a wholly different trajectory representation (B-spline, learned VIO frontend, per-frame visual tag detection).

STUCK: 30 consecutive non-kept iterations from baseline kp[A=22.5 G=49.8 T=38.6] anc[med=18.8 max=191.3] (commit b341685). Pattern across 30 reverts:

1. Filter approaches (SVD-conditioning, reproj-residual, parallax filter, min_track_length tightening) — improve anc_med dramatically (-50% to -90%) but regress kp_T 40-150%
2. Bridge approaches (ORB-bridge, ORB-bridge with chain-snap, AKAZE-bridge, IMU-rotation-consistency-filtered AKAZE-bridge, chain-revival ORB-bridge) — bridges add noisy triangulation, kp_T regresses
3. New factor types (anchor-center reprojection at σ=3 and σ=10) — even at very loose σ, perturbs LM
4. IMU integration boundary fixes (gyro and preintegrate) — math-correct fix but regresses kp_T (LM at sharp local optimum sensitive to measurements)
5. IMU rotation predictor full-pose (rotation+translation) — crashed (gravity unknown pre-VI-align)
6. Per-track adaptive σ (4 knobs) — non-monotonic with cap; trade-off between kp_A and kp_G/anc
7. FD velocity init at anchor kfs — catastrophic (different from VI-LSQ, IMU forward-prop diverges)
8. BRISK seeds for KLT — different track set, kp_T +154%
9. Disabled IMU rotation predictor — confirms predictor IS load-bearing for kp_T (regression)
10. Defensive bug fixes (IPPE temporal disambiguation in finger PnP, chain-revival bridge) — exactly-baseline metrics, no improvement

Conclusion: kp_T=38.6 baseline is at a brittle local optimum where every existing measurement is load-bearing. Within focus-mode constraints (no σ tuning of anchor/IMU/cross-tracker/smoothness, no LM stage modifications, no between-pose factor add/remove, no metric-derived priors), no path produces ≥5% improvement on any segment without >5% regression on another. The trade-off between segments is a fundamental Pareto front. Escalation paths blocked by focus mode would be: σ tuning of cross-tracker (forbidden), multi-stage LM with iter re-tri (forbidden), B-spline trajectory parametrization (forbidden in visual-frontend-only mode), inter-tracker keypoint smoothness (cheating).

Recommend: re-evaluate focus-mode constraints OR accept current kp_T=38.6 as data-imposed bound for this clip pair.

The Mode B 5% gates filter all sweeps. Path forward likely requires fundamentally new architecture: SuperPoint+LightGlue (needs torch install) or visual-IMU coupling outside frontend (forbidden under focus mode).
